I mean, my dad’s results are the ones I’ve been watching, and that’s where the chart doesn’t match what I saw. I’m not saying I know it’s some huge thing with everybody, but… clinic systems are messy, you know? I work around blood draws, and people act like paperwork and labels and charts are just magically correct every time.\n\nBut for the past year, I’ve been trying to prove that something is being changed or held back with his labs specifically. And every time I bring it up, doctors and my family say the numbers are fine, like that should settle it. It doesn’t, because I know what I saw. It’s been wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that you might be mistaken about the lab results?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’ve tried to ask myself that, because everyone keeps saying it like I’m just stressed or reading it wrong. But then I come back to the same thing: I know what I saw, and the chart later didn’t match it.\n\nAnd it’s not like I’m some random person who’s never been around labs. I’m a phlebotomist, I know how results and samples are supposed to work—well, mostly, I mean there’s always paperwork stuff, and people rushing, and computers being slow, and at my job there are days where the labels pile up and you just think, how does anything stay straight? But with my dad’s labs, this has been going on for a year. I keep trying to prove someone in the clinic system is changing something or holding parts back, and doctors and my family keep saying the numbers are fine.\n\nSo… mistaken? I guess I’ve considered it because people keep pushing that on me. But I don’t believe that’s what this is. It’s wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Would you change your mind about someone altering your dad's lab results if you were shown proof that the results are accurate?\n\nPatient: If it was real proof, I mean… I’d want to see it myself, not just have someone tell me, “It’s fine.” That’s what keeps happening, and then I’m supposed to just drop it.\n\nBecause I’m here because for the past year I’ve been trying to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results. And everyone keeps saying the numbers are fine—doctors, my family—but the chart doesn’t match what I saw. It just doesn’t.\n\nSo would I change my mind? I don’t know. Maybe if I could really see everything from start to finish, like the actual process, not just a printout after the fact. At work, I’ve seen how things can get missed when people are rushing, and then everyone acts like the computer is the truth. That’s the part that makes me feel like I can’t just accept it. It’s been wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that no amount of evidence could ever change your mind about someone altering your dad's lab results?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say no amount, exactly… but it would have to be more than people just telling me I’m wrong. Because that’s what keeps happening, and then they act like I’m being difficult.\n\nI’m here because for the past year I’ve been trying to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results. And I know everyone says the numbers are fine—doctors, my family—but I saw something different, and then the chart didn’t match. That’s not nothing.\n\nAt work, even with little things, labels, tubes, computers… people think it’s all clean and perfect, but it isn’t always. So when they just show me the chart after the fact, I can’t just accept that as proof. It’s been wearing me down, honestly. So… I guess I’m not saying impossible, but I don’t know what would make me fully believe them unless I could really see how it all happened.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would ever be able to accept that your dad's lab results are accurate, even if multiple people and evidence confirm it?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, if multiple people said it, that’s already kind of what’s been happening, right? Doctors say it, my family says it, and I’m supposed to just nod and be relieved, but I can’t get past what I saw.\n\nFor the past year I’ve been trying to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing something or holding parts of my dad’s labs back. And I know that sounds exhausting—because it is, it’s completely wearing me down—but the chart doesn’t match what I remember seeing. At work, even just with regular draws, things get messy, labels, computers, people rushing… and then everyone acts like once it’s in the system it’s untouchable.\n\nSo could I ever accept it? Maybe I want to say yes, because I don’t want to keep living like this. But if the “evidence” is just more people showing me the same chart and telling me the numbers are fine, then no, I don’t think I could just accept that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility, no matter how small, that you could be wrong about your dad's lab results being altered?\n\nPatient: I mean… a possibility? I guess technically people can be wrong. I’m not trying to say I’m perfect or that I remember every single number like a machine. But then I go back to what I saw, and the chart just doesn’t line up with it.\n\nThat’s the part everyone skips over. They say, “The doctors reviewed it,” or my family says, “You need to let this go,” but for the past year I’ve been trying to prove that someone in that clinic system changed something or held part of my dad’s labs back. And I know how clinics are, I work around blood draws and paperwork and computers, and people act like the system can’t be touched once it’s entered, but that’s not real life.\n\nSo… could I be wrong in some tiny way? Maybe. But I don’t feel wrong. I feel like I’m the only one not pretending the chart matches what I saw, and it’s wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think about your dad's lab results being altered every day?\n\nPatient: Every day? Yeah… I mean, maybe not every single minute, but it’s there every day. I’ll be doing something normal, like at work with tubes or labels, and then I start thinking about how easy it is for something to not match later.\n\nAnd then with my dad’s labs, that’s the thing—I’ve spent basically the past year trying to prove someone in that clinic system changed something or held part of it back. People keep saying the numbers are fine, the doctors checked it, my family says I’m wearing myself out, but the chart doesn’t match what I saw.\n\nSo yes, I think about it every day. It’s exhausting.\n\nDoctor: Would your belief about your dad's lab results being altered change if you were given a detailed explanation of how the lab results are handled and verified?\n\nPatient: I mean, a detailed explanation might help me understand the process, sure. I know there are checks and steps and people sign off on things—I’m a phlebotomist, I’ve seen how much of it is supposed to be tracked. But that’s also why it bothers me, because I know systems still have people in them, and people can change things or leave things out.\n\nAnd I keep coming back to the same thing, even if everyone wants me to move on: I’m here because for the past year I’ve been trying to prove someone in that clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results. Doctors, family, everybody keeps telling me the numbers are fine, but the chart doesn’t match what I saw.\n\nSo would an explanation change it? I don’t know. Maybe it would calm me for a little while, but if it doesn’t explain that mismatch, then no… I don’t think it would really change what I believe. It’s wearing me down, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your dad's lab results would ever change, no matter what evidence or explanations you receive?\n\nPatient: I don’t know how to answer “ever,” because that feels like you’re asking me to promise I’d ignore proof, and I’m not trying to be unreasonable. I work with lab stuff, you know, tubes and labels and people initialing things, and sometimes people act like computers make everything clean, but half the time it’s still a person clicking the button.\n\nBut with my dad’s chart… I keep coming back to it. For the past year I’ve been trying to prove that someone in that clinic system changed something or held parts of his labs back. And no matter how many doctors tell me the numbers are fine, or my family tells me I’m exhausting myself, I know what I saw. The chart doesn’t match that.\n\nSo… if someone could actually explain that, maybe. But if it’s just more “trust us, it’s fine,” then no, I don’t think it would change. It’s worn me down too much to just drop it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there will ever be a point where you stop thinking about your dad's lab results being altered?\n\nPatient: I want there to be a point. I really do, because I’m tired. Like, I’ll be drawing blood at work and I’ll see how a label prints or how someone sets something down for just a second, and my brain goes right back to it—even if I was thinking about groceries or my car needing an oil change or whatever.\n\nBut I don’t know how I stop when nobody has actually explained the part that doesn’t match. I’m here because for the past year I’ve been trying to prove someone in that clinic system changed something or held back parts of my dad’s lab results. And everyone—doctors, my family—keeps telling me the numbers are fine, but I know what I saw in the chart. It doesn’t match.\n\nSo… maybe if that got answered for real, maybe I could stop. But right now, no. It’s still there, and it’s wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your dad's lab results being altered is based on facts or is it just a feeling you have?\n\nPatient: It’s facts to me. I know people keep trying to make it sound like it’s just anxiety or me getting stuck on something, but I’m not just making it up out of nowhere.\n\nI work around lab processes, so I know how things are supposed to look, and I know charts don’t just magically feel “off.” There was something that didn’t match what I saw. And then everyone—doctors, my family—keeps saying the numbers are fine, like repeating that is supposed to erase it.\n\nI mean, I get that I probably sound… intense about it. My sister says I go in circles, and maybe I do. I’ll be at work and see a requisition or a label and then I’m thinking about my dad’s chart again instead of what I’m supposed to be doing. But for the past year I’ve been trying to prove someone in that clinic system changed or held back part of his lab results. That’s not just a feeling. The chart doesn’t match what I saw, and I can’t just ignore that. It’s wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your dad's lab results would change if you were shown undeniable proof that they are accurate?\n\nPatient: If it was really undeniable, I’d like to say yes. I mean, I’m not trying to be the kind of person who just refuses reality. I work with blood draws and labels and all that, so I know there are procedures, but I also know how easy it is for one little thing to get clicked wrong or filed wrong, and then everyone just keeps moving like nothing happened.\n\nSo when you say “undeniable proof,” I start thinking, proof from who? The same clinic system? Because that’s where the problem is. I’ve spent the past year trying to prove someone changed something or held back parts of my dad’s lab results, and everyone keeps telling me, “The numbers are fine,” my family included, but they don’t understand what I saw in the chart. It didn’t match.\n\nMaybe if someone could sit down and actually explain every part of it in a way that made sense, maybe. But if it’s just another printout or another doctor saying it’s accurate, I don’t know. I don’t think I could just let it go. It’s worn me down too much.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your dad's lab results being altered is something that you might be mistaken about, even if you're completely convinced it's true right now?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess anyone can be mistaken, technically. Like, I’ve mislabeled a tube before and caught it right away, so I know people make mistakes both ways. But with this, it doesn’t feel like just me being stubborn.\n\nI’ve been trying for the past year to prove that someone in that clinic system changed something or held back parts of my dad’s lab results. That’s the part I keep coming back to.\n\nSo could I be mistaken? I don’t know. Maybe in some broad sense, yes. But right now I’m completely convinced something isn’t right, and it’s… it’s wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could happen, or any information you could receive, that would make you completely change your mind about your dad's lab results being altered?\n\nPatient: I don’t know what would make me completely change my mind. Maybe if someone outside that whole clinic system could go through it with me, not just hand me another page and say, “See, it’s fine.” But even then, I’d have questions, because the chart did not match what I saw.\n\nAnd I know I keep saying that, but it’s because that’s the part nobody answers. For the past year I’ve been trying to prove that someone in that clinic system changed something or held parts of his labs back. And no matter how many doctors tell me the numbers are fine, or my family tells me I’m exhausting myself, I know what I saw. The chart doesn’t match that.\n\nSo… if someone could actually explain that, maybe. But if it’s just more “trust us, it’s fine,” then no, I don’t think it would change. It’s worn me down too much to just drop it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there will ever be a point where you stop thinking about your dad's lab results being altered?\n\nPatient: I want there to be a point. I really do, because I’m tired. Like, I’ll be drawing blood at work and I’ll see how a label prints or how someone sets something down for just a second, and my brain goes right back to it—even if I was thinking about groceries or my car needing an oil change or whatever.\n\nBut I don’t know how I stop when nobody has actually explained the part that doesn’t match. I’m here because for the past year I’ve been trying to prove someone in that clinic system changed something or held back parts of my dad’s lab results. But right now, no. It’s still there, and it’s wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your dad's lab results being altered is based on facts or is it just a feeling you have?\n\nPatient: It’s facts to me. And then everyone—doctors, my family—keeps saying the numbers are fine, like repeating that is supposed to erase it.\n\nI mean, I get that I probably sound… intense about it. I work with blood draws and labels and all that, so I know there are procedures, but I also know how easy it is for one little thing to get clicked wrong or filed wrong, and then everyone just keeps moving like nothing happened.\n\nSo when you say “undeniable proof,” I start thinking, proof from who? It’s worn me down too much.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your dad's lab results being altered is something that you might be mistaken about, even if you're completely convinced it's true right now?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess anyone can be mistaken, technically.
